     3. Key design decisions (recommendation, why)

     D1 Migrations vs Prisma. Migrations are the source of truth; schema.prisma 
     is generated. Needed for RLS, grants, triggers, the exclusion constraint
     and the bucket, none of which Prisma Migrate models. Sync: npm run db:sync
     = supabase db reset && prisma db pull && prisma generate, never hand-edit
     schema.prisma, commit it after every migration change. db pull cannot
     express the EXCLUDE/CHECKs; that is fine (they live in SQL), and I will
     verify the pull is clean in Phase 2. Prisma is used only for server-side
     data access (as owner, bypassing RLS).

     D2 Seed. scripts/seed.ts (run by tsx --env-file=.env.local) calls the same 
     service functions the API uses, with an Actor object, not HTTP. Users via
     supabase.auth.admin.createUser({ email_confirm: true, password }) with the
     secret key; memberships/tenants/units via Prisma; leases through
     createLease; payments through recordPayment (manager ⇒ approved, staff ⇒
     pending) then approvePayment, so invoices, allocations and audit rows are
     exactly what the app produces. Slip PNG uploaded with upsert: true (db
     reset does not clear storage volumes). Refuses to run if orgs already exist
     (prints "run npx supabase db reset"), so a double run cannot create
     duplicates.

     D3 Auth helper. One getActor(req) in src/lib/auth.ts. If an Authorization: 
     Bearer header exists, validate it with getUser(token) using a stateless
     client built from the publishable key, and a bad token is 401 with no 
     cookie fallback. Otherwise use the @supabase/ssr cookie client's getUser().
     getUser over getClaims because it checks with the Auth server (sees
     revoked users) and the local Auth is fast. Then role lookup: memberships by
     user_id ⇒ staff/manager + orgId; else tenants by user_id ⇒ tenant +
     tenantId + orgId; valid login with neither ⇒ 403. Fixed pipeline in every
     handler: authenticate (401) → load the record through a visibility-scoped 
     finder (404; malformed UUID is also 404) → role check (403) → body 
     validation (400) → state check (409) → cap/balance (422). The finder scopes
     by org_id for staff/manager and by tenant_id for tenants, so cross-org and
     cross-tenant are indistinguishable from "missing". route() wrapper maps
     HttpError to {error} JSON. Server Actions and server components call the
     same getActor.

     D4 Concurrency and overlap. Lock the lease row. Every money-changing
     operation (record+cap, approve, reject, void, webhook allocation) runs in
     one prisma.$transaction, first statement SELECT id FROM leases WHERE id = 
     $1 FOR UPDATE, then re-reads the payment/bill status inside the lock and
     guards the transition. The cap sum, the approve re-check, void, and
     allocation therefore all serialize per lease, and "approve twice" allocates
     once (second waits, sees approved, returns 409). Webhook lock order is
     bill → lease; nothing locks lease → bill, so no deadlock. Business logic
     stays in TypeScript services (explainable line by line) instead of PL/pgSQL
     functions, which would also be new rpc surface to lock down. Overlap:
     EXCLUDE USING gist (unit_id WITH =, daterange(start_date, end_date, '[]') 
     WITH &&): the database rejects the second concurrent insert (SQLSTATE 23P01
     ⇒ 409), no app-level lock needed. Backstops: UNIQUE(payment_id, 
     invoice_id) on allocations.

     D5 Webhook idempotency. Row lock plus unique index. Order: parse raw body
     with URLSearchParams (duplicate keys ⇒ treated as bad signature) → verify
     HMAC (constant time; lengths checked first) ⇒ 401 → read id → tx: SELECT 
     ... FROM gateway_bills WHERE id=$1 FOR UPDATE (none ⇒ 404) → paid=true:
     amount mismatch ⇒ 422; paid ⇒ 200 no-op; else lock lease, insert the
     approved online payment (gateway_bill_id, received_at = paid_at parsed with
     its offset), allocate, set bill paid, audit → paid=false: only pending → 
     failed. A second concurrent delivery blocks on the bill lock, then sees
     paid and returns 200. UNIQUE(payments.gateway_bill_id) is the structural
     guarantee ("at most one payment, ever"); a 23505 is treated as a replay
     (200), never 500. Logging: only bill id + outcome codes; no body, no key.

     D6 Data API lockdown. Deny by default, grant narrowly, helpers off the API 
     surface. REVOKE ALL on public tables from PUBLIC, anon, authenticated
     (service_role keeps its grants, per Q7), then GRANT SELECT to authenticated
     only on the 9 readable tables, plus column-level UPDATE (phone) on
     tenants. Defaults are neutralised by revoking after creation in the same
     migration (plus ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES for the migration role). Policies
     TO authenticated, built on private.* SECURITY DEFINER helpers (avoids
     recursive RLS). authenticated gets USAGE on schema private and EXECUTE on
     the helpers (RLS needs both); they are still not callable via /rest/v1/rpc
     because nothing lives in public and private is not in api.schemas. EXECUTE
     is revoked from PUBLIC and anon only. A probe test proves rpc calls to each
     private.* helper fail. Tenant phone update = column grant + RLS user_id = 
     auth.uid() (full_name/email/user_id/org_id updates fail with permission
     denied). audit_log: triggers on UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE raise for everyone
     (owner and service_role included); UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE also revoked from
     anon/authenticated; INSERT only by Prisma/owner. service_role keeps table
     privileges (Q7): the tests use the publishable key + user tokens and the
     secret key never leaves the server, so the triggers are what make audit_log
     append-only for it.

     D7 Storage and cleanup. Bucket in a migration: private, 5 MB, jpeg/png/pdf.
     Policies: INSERT and SELECT for staff/managers whose org id equals the
     first path segment; no UPDATE/DELETE, no tenant or anon policy. Signed URLs
     (60 s) created with the user-scoped client, so the policy is a second
     enforcement layer. Upload happens first (client, user token), then POST 
     .../payments carries slipPath. Server validation: path starts with
     <actor.orgId>/, no .., and a storage.objects row exists for the bucket
     (also re-checks size and mimetype). Cleanup: in the route's failure path,
     after the caller is authenticated as staff/manager, if slipPath is in the
     caller's own org folder and no payment references it, delete the object
     with the admin client. UNIQUE(slip_path) plus "only delete if unreferenced"
     means a failed request can never delete another payment's slip, and the
     loser of a concurrent same-slip race does not delete the winner's file.

     D8 Time. Everything derives from three primitives in pure code:
     malaysiaDate(now) (billing.ts) for "today", parseOffsetInstant for incoming
     instants (rejects strings without an offset), SQL AT TIME ZONE 
     'Asia/Kuala_Lumpur' for month bucketing (report range = [first-of-from 
     00:00 MYT, first-of-(to+1) 00:00 MYT), zero months filled in TS from
     monthRange). UI: formatMyt is pure arithmetic with no Intl/locale, so
     server and client render identical strings; datetime-local text is combined
     with +08:00 rather than parsed by the browser; no new Date()/Date.now() in
     render paths. This also makes DECISIONS Q3 a short list.

     D9 Money in TS. DB bigint ⇒ Prisma BigInt; services do arithmetic in BigInt
     or in SQL (SUM); a single senToNumber() at the API boundary asserts
     Number.isSafeInteger. billing.ts keeps the contract's number fields
     (integers only) and uses BigInt for proration.

     ---

     4. Risks and fallbacks

     Risk: Prisma install/generate hang again
     Impact: Blocks Phases 0, 2
     Mitigation / fallback: Pinned 7.10.1, foreground logs, 3-min stall rule,
     --ignore-scripts retry. Fallback: use postgres/pg raw SQL in services for
     the
      locking-critical paths, keep Prisma only for introspection.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: Prisma 7 API differs from my memory (config file, adapter, generator
     output)
     Impact: 10 min lost
     Mitigation / fallback: Read installed package docs in Phase 0 before
     writing
     prisma.ts.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: db pull mangles auth FKs, enums, exclusion constraint
     Impact: schema.prisma mismatch
     Mitigation / fallback: Spike in Phase 2 start; if FK to auth.users confuses

     Prisma, keep column plain uuid and rely on seed/server checks.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: RLS/grant mistake leaks data (25 pts + IF2)
     Impact: Fatal
     Mitigation / fallback: Probe test over every role × table × verb × rpc is
     part
     of Phase 2; re-run in Phases 5 and 7.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: Concurrency tests flaky
     Impact: Time sink
     Mitigation / fallback: Use Promise.all of N requests plus assert on final
     DB
     state, not timing.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: Next 16 cacheComponents build errors on dynamic pages
     Impact: UI phase blow-up
     Mitigation / fallback: Open Q2 (remove flags). Fallback export const 
     instant = 
     false per page.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: shadcn CLI network stall
     Impact: UI delay
     Mitigation / fallback: Only init in P0, components added in P6; fallback
     copy
     components by hand.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: TZ test setup on Windows
     Impact: False greens
     Mitigation / fallback: test-tz.mjs prints getTimezoneOffset() in each run;
     never rely on bash TZ=.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Risk: Overrun
     Impact: Missing write-ups
     Mitigation / fallback: Hard stop at 3:05; write-ups are pre-planned
     (Appendix
     A).

     Cut list, in order (brief: a correct secure subset beats a complete sloppy
     app): bonuses → UI polish/empty states → tenant portal polish (keep phone
     edit + pay now) → composite FKs and extra constraints beyond the
     exclusion/uniques → /payments/return styling → leases list pagination.
     Never cut: IF1–IF4, RLS/grants/audit trigger, the 401/403/404 pipeline,
     lease locking, webhook table + unique index, billing tests, README
     "skipped" section.

     ---

     5. Open questions (answer before we start; each has my default)

     1. Seed password for all 7 accounts (goes in .env-adjacent docs, README,
        submission). Needs your value.
     2. cacheComponents: true / partialPrefetching: true in next.config.ts:
        remove? Default: remove both (every page here is per-user dynamic; flags
        add Suspense/prerender rules and build-error risk; the brief does not
        need them). Keep the Tailwind turbopack loader.
     3. Branch/remote: rename master → main? Who creates the public GitHub repo
        and what is the remote URL? Default: rename to main; I never push until 
        you say so.
     4. Webhook gaps (brief is silent): (a) paid=true but transaction_status is
        failed/pending: default: ignore transaction_status, trust paid. (b)
        paid=true with wrong paid_amount on an already-paid bill: default: 422 
        (amount check before status). (c) missing/unparseable paid_at or
        non-numeric paid_amount with a valid signature: default: 400.
     5. Void of an online payment that still has unapplied_sen (DECISIONS Q5):
        default: do nothing to other payments (brief: their allocations do not 
        change), leave unapplied credit as is, and leave the voided payment's 
        own unapplied_sen untouched. Alternative: zero it on void or
        auto-reallocate. Your call.
     6. Manager-recorded payment (approved immediately): write an audit_log row
        payment.approved too? Default: yes (details mark auto: true).
     7. service_role on public tables via Data API: default: revoke everything
        ("every other insert/update/delete must fail for everyone"). Risk: a
        hidden test that inserts an audit row with the service key as setup
        would fail.
     8. /payments/return: require login and show "not found" unless the bill
        belongs to the viewer (tenant of the lease, or staff/manager of its
        org)? Default: yes. Alternative: public by unguessable bill id, status
        only.
     9. Prisma version: default: 7.10.1 for CLI, client and adapter (not the 
        8.0.0-rc.28 that latest resolves to).
     10. Defaults I will apply unless you object: cross-org or unknown
         unitId/tenantId/leaseId in a body or path ⇒ 404 (CLAUDE.md rule);
         reason is trimmed before the 5-char test; cash with a slipPath ⇒ 400;
         receivedAt without an offset ⇒ 400; amountSen must be a positive safe
         integer ⇒ 400; valid login with no membership/tenant row ⇒ 403;
         collections range has no maximum span, from > to or bad format ⇒ 400;
         invalid UUID in a path ⇒ 404.

     ---

     Appendix A: REVIEW.md prep (Part B)

     ▎ Correction B: every L### below is a SPEC.md line number, NOT a line 
     ▎ number inside the snippet file. In Phase 7, re-count from 1 within each 
     ▎ of the three files (late-fees/route.ts, webhooks/gateway/route.ts, 
     ▎ dashboard/actions.ts) and use only those numbers in REVIEW.md. (Offsets, 
     ▎ with the // app/... path comment counted as line 1: snippet 1 starts at 
     ▎ SPEC.md 389 ⇒ SPEC L401 = line 13; snippet 2 starts at SPEC.md 443; 
     ▎ snippet 3 at SPEC.md 485. REVIEW.md will state this convention at the 
     ▎ top, and I will recount each cited line against the snippet text rather 
     ▎ than trusting the offset arithmetic.)

     Ordered most → least severe. Only real problems; "looks wrong but fine"
     listed separately.

     late-fees/route.ts
     1. Critical L401: role read from user_metadata (user-editable) ⇒ any user
        escalates to manager. Fix: role from memberships.
     2. Critical L406: no org/ownership check on leaseId; Prisma bypasses RLS ⇒
        a manager of org B adds fees to org A's lease (IDOR). Fix: scope lookup
        by actor's org ⇒ 404.
     3. High L415–434: not idempotent (second call adds the same fees again), no
        transaction (partial writes on failure), no lease lock (concurrent
        calls duplicate).
     4. High L429/L438–440: reference: await nextReference() is resolved before
        any create runs (Prisma queries are lazy), so every invoice in the loop
        gets the same INV-n; also read-then-write race and parseInt of a
        null/other-format reference gives INV-NaN.
     5. Medium L421: kind: 'late_fee' is outside the allowed invoice kinds
        (deposit/rent) and breaks the lease balance/cap model.
     6. Medium L419–420: money via floats (Number(...)/Number(...) >= 0.995
        silently forgives up to 0.5%; Math.round(x*2/100)). Fix: BigInt integer
        math, explicit rule.
     7. Low L401–403: unauthenticated returns 403, should be 401; L427 new 
        Date(todayMyt) fine (UTC midnight) but only works because Prisma reads
        DATE as UTC.

     webhooks/gateway/route.ts
     1. Critical L458: logs fields and the expected signature ⇒ anyone with log
        access can forge valid callbacks (and logs whole payload, forbidden).
     2. High L463–475: read-then-write race, no lock; two concurrent deliveries
        both see pending ⇒ double payment (or 500 on unique). Fix: FOR UPDATE on
        bill row in the transaction + unique gateway_bill_id.
     3. High L461: paid !== 'true' returns 200 before the bill lookup ⇒ unknown
        id never 404, pending bill never becomes failed.
     4. High L475: BigInt(fields.paid_amount) throws on junk (500) and is never
        compared to bill.amountSen ⇒ attacker or gateway bug settles a bill with
        any amount (422 required).
     5. Medium L476: receivedAt: new Date() instead of paid_at ⇒ wrong month in
        collections.
     6. Medium L457: !== string compare is not constant-time; ! on a missing env
        var gives a 500.
        (Former point 7, "allocation without locking the lease", deleted at
        review: the brief says to assume allocate(tx, paymentId) is correct, so
        it is out of scope for REVIEW.md. Our own webhook still locks bill →
        lease before calling allocate, see D5.)

     dashboard/actions.ts
     1. Critical L490: 'use server' export is a public endpoint; orgId comes
        from the client, no authentication/authorization ⇒ any caller (including
        tenants/anon) reads any org's collections.
     2. High L492–495: date_trunc('month', received_at) and ::date comparisons
        use the DB session timezone (UTC) ⇒ payments between 00:00–08:00 MYT on
        the 1st land in the previous month, range edges shifted 8 h. Fix: AT 
        TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kuala_Lumpur' on both.
     3. Medium L497: months with no payments are omitted (brief requires zero
        rows); no validation of from/to (bad input ⇒ 500).
     4. Looks suspicious but fine: the tagged-template $queryRaw is
        parameterised (no SQL injection); Number(r.total) is safe within the
        safe-integer range (type annotation bigint is wrong, since SUM returns
        numeric, but the conversion still works).

  Verification

  - npm run typecheck and npm run lint pass.
  - vitest runs (v5.0.5, no tests yet).
  - npm run build passes.
  - prisma -v reports 7.10.1 with the schema engine present. prisma generate
    works with no env file, the fresh-clone postinstall case.
  - grep of .next/static for sb_secret, SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY and service_role
    finds nothing.

  Findings

  1. The install hang was slow npm, not a Prisma bug. Registry fetches took
     7–84s each. The first install took 7m27s. The Prisma CLI install finished
     in about 3 minutes with no stall. The earlier problem was probably the
     prisma latest tag resolving to 8.0.0-rc.28 against client 7.10.1.
  2. @types/node bumped from ^20 to ^24.19.2. vitest@5 needs ^22 || >=24, and
     Node here is v24.
  3. prisma init writes agent-skill folders and skills-lock.json. I ran it only
     in a scratch directory and hand-wrote the Prisma files instead.
  4. shadcn added a cn package. Its repo is shadcn-ui/cn, maintained by shadcn.
     utils.ts is just export { cn } from "cn".
  5. Prisma 7 config has no directUrl. DIRECT_URL feeds the CLI and DATABASE_URL
     feeds the runtime adapter.
  6. Supabase has an auto_expose_new_tables option in config.toml. I didn't
     change it. We can decide in Phase 2 whether to set it to false as
     deny-by-default alongside the explicit revokes.

  Decision for you

  .env.example contains the local-stack sb_secret_… key. The brief asks for
  local values, and that key is identical on every local Supabase stack, so it
  isn't a real secret. But GitHub push protection may flag the pattern when you
  push. Options:
  - Keep as is. It matches the brief literally.
  - Use a placeholder. .env.example would then need a comment telling graders to
    fill in the key from npx supabase status, which they say they do anyway.

  I haven't changed anything and the commit is local, so either is cheap. Which
  do you want?

  Stopping here for your check. Next is Phase 1 (billing, tests first) once you
  say go.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
● Phase 1 is committed locally as 797f259 and nothing is pushed. origin points
  at https://github.com/ZenatyAh/ezy-rent.git. The re-plan is 7246d57.

  What changed

  - src/lib/billing.ts (pure, no imports) exports generateSchedule,
    invoiceStatus, parseRinggitToSen and malaysiaDate. malaysiaDate is the
    single place the app derives "today" from an instant, so DECISIONS Q3 stays
    short.
  - Date math uses UTC day numbers and setUTCFullYear; I avoided Date.UTC
    because it maps years 0–99 to 1900–1999. A date is valid if it survives a
    round trip: 2026-02-30 comes back as 2026-03-02, so it's rejected.
  - Anchors are always counted from the start date and clamped to month length,
    so Jan 31 → Feb 28/29 → Mar 31.
  - Proration is floor((2·rent·used + full) / (2·full)) in BigInt, which is
    exact round-half-up with no float error.
  - src/lib/billing.test.ts has 119 cases, written first and seen failing. They
    cover the worked example, all four seeded leases, validation, the overdue
    boundary and parseRinggitToSen.
  - TZ tooling: scripts/test-tz.mjs runs the suite under the three timezones.
    src/lib/tz-guard.test.ts fails a run if TZ didn't actually apply.
  - tsconfig target went from ES2017 to ES2020 so BigInt literals compile. This
    only affects type-checking.

  Verification

  - npm test: 120/120 pass.
  - npm run test:tz: 120/120 under UTC, Asia/Kuala_Lumpur and America/New_York.
  - Grep of billing.ts for imports, local-time getters, Date.now and date-string
    constructors finds none.
  - npm run typecheck, npm run lint and npm run build are clean.

  Notes

  1. One failure, and it was my test. I wrote the large-rent test starting on
     06-30, which makes the next anchor Jul 30 (30 days), not 31. The code was
     right. I fixed the test to start on 2026-07-31. This is the first entry for
     docs/notes.md, but I haven't logged it there yet.
  2. 0-sen invoices are possible. The brief's formula can round a tiny last
     period to 0 sen (1 sen of rent for 1 of 31 days). I followed the formula,
     with a test that says so. So invoices.amount_sen gets CHECK (>= 0) in Phase
     2, not > 0. This is recorded in docs/PLAN.md.
  3. Year 0000-01-01 is accepted. It round-trips in JS but Postgres DATE can't
     store it. It's irrelevant in practice and I left it.

  Phase 1 took about 14 minutes of its 20-minute box, so the timer should show
  roughly 2h42m. Stopping here for your check. Phase 2 (schema, RLS, grants,
  storage, audit trigger, Data API probe) is next.

      480 +// Short reason for a failed Storage call, so a denial can be 
          +told apart from a malformed request.                          
      481 +function why(text: string): string {                          
      482 +  const message = (() => {                                    
      483 +    try {                                                     
      484 +      return String((JSON.parse(text) as { message?: unknown }
          +).message ?? text)                                            
      485 +    } catch {                                                 
      486 +      return text                                             
      487 +    }                                                         
      488 +  })().toLowerCase()                                          
      489 +  if (message.includes('row-level security')) return 'RLS'    
      490 +  if (message.includes('mime')) return 'mime type'            
      491 +  if (message.includes('maximum allowed size') || message.incl
          +udes('too large')) return 'size'                              
      492 +  if (message.includes('not found')) return 'not found'       
      493 +  if (message.includes('jwt') || message.includes('authorizati
          +on')) return 'auth'                                           
      494 +  return message.slice(0, 24)                                 
      495 +}                                                             
      496 +const deny = (r: { status: number; text: string }): string => 
          +`HTTP ${r.status} deny [${why(r.text)}]`                      
      497 +

● Phase 2 is committed: 345c828 for the DB work and c1f8545 for the saved probe
  output. The probe is 478 checks, 0 failures, and the DB constraint checks are
  26/26. Nothing is pushed.

  Wall-clock was about 18 minutes (11:25 to 11:43) against a 35-minute box. That
  banks about 17 minutes, which goes to the UI phase as planned.

  What was built

  - Four migrations: schema, audit append-only, security, storage.
  - Schema safeguards:
    - Composite (id, org_id) foreign keys, so no row can point at another
      organization's parent.
    - An EXCLUDE USING gist constraint for lease overlap, with inclusive dates.
    - CHECKs: invoices.amount_sen >= 0, payment_allocations.amount_sen > 0, and
      a slip path that must be inside the org's folder.
  - Access control: revoke-then-grant, with anon getting nothing. authenticated
    gets SELECT only, plus UPDATE (phone) on tenants. private.* helpers get
    USAGE and EXECUTE for authenticated only, per your correction A.
    auto_expose_new_tables = false is set.
  - Audit log: append-only by statement- and row-level triggers, so it holds
    even when zero rows match.
  - Storage: private payment-slips bucket (5 MB; jpeg/png/pdf). Insert and
    select policies are scoped to the org's staff and managers, and there are no
    update or delete policies.
  - Tooling:
    - schema.prisma is generated by npm run db:sync with no hand edits.
    - src/lib/prisma.ts is the server-only Prisma client.
    - Seed stage 1 creates orgs, 7 auth users, memberships, tenants and units,
      and refuses a second run.
    - New scripts: npm run probe, npm run check:db, npm run seed.

  allocation, 2 bills, 2 audit rows) and I then reset and re-seeded. Expected
  counts come from SQL against the database, not hard-coded. Full output is in
  docs/probe-phase2.txt.

  probe's uploads were cleaned up, leaving 0 objects.

  Things you should know

  1. I made three design changes to my own plan, found by running things (logged
     in docs/notes.md):
     - The FKs to auth.users are gone because Prisma can't introspect
       cross-schema references. The user_id columns are plain uuid. Deleting an
       auth user no longer cascades, and an orphaned membership grants nothing.
     - The partial unique index became UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (lease_id, 
       period_index), which also means one deposit per lease.
     - tenants_update_own now has WITH CHECK, so staff updates fail loudly.
  2. Prisma models use the exact table and column names (leases, org_id,
     amount_sen). Domain code will read lease.org_id. I chose zero hand edits
     over camelCase.
  3. service_role can still write to non-audit tables through the Data API, per
     your Q7. The probe doesn't assert that either way.
  4. A tenant reading memberships or audit_log gets 0 rows, not an error. That's
     how a shared authenticated role works. The brief's "none" is satisfied,
     but a hidden test that expects an error there would differ.
  5. Not covered yet:
     - A user with a valid login but no membership or tenant row. There's no
       such account in the seed, so Phase 3's API tests will cover it
       (expecting 403).
     - Server-side authorization, which is Phase 3.
     - Re-running the probe on real seeded data. After Phase 4's seed, plain npm
       run probe should pass with no fixtures.

  Stopping here for your check. Next is Phase 3 (auth helper, leases API, seed
  stage 2), including the 400 for dates Postgres can't store.

● Phase 3 is committed as a037b18 on main. Nothing is pushed. It took about 27
  minutes of wall-clock against a 15-minute box, so about 5 minutes remain
  banked overall (17 from Phase 2, minus 12 here). Two detours cost most of the
  overrun; they're under Findings.

  What was built

  - Auth helper (src/lib/auth.ts):
    - getActor() authenticates with auth.getUser() and accepts a Bearer token or
      the session cookie.
    - A bad Bearer header is a 401, with no fallback to the cookie.
    - The role and org come only from memberships or tenants.user_id. A valid
      login with neither gets 403.
  - Shared plumbing:
    - route() turns HttpError and Zod errors into { "error": … }.
    - requireRole() handles the 403 checks.
    - src/proxy.ts only refreshes the session cookie on page requests; it is not
      an authorization layer.
  - POST /api/leases: the check order is 401 → 403 → 400 → 404 → 409.
    - The unit and tenant are looked up inside the caller's org, so another
      org's ids look like unknown ones (404).
    - Overlap is rejected by the database constraint (409).
    - One transaction writes the lease, exactly the generateSchedule invoices,
      and a lease.created audit row.
    - Year 0000 returns 400, not 500 (your note 3 from Phase 1).
  - GET /api/leases/:leaseId: staff and managers of the org plus the lease's own
    tenant. Everyone else gets 404. Invoices come back in allocation order.
  - Seed stage 2: the four leases go through createLease as the real manager
    actors, giving 37 invoices and 4 audit rows.
  - Helpers: scripts/token.mjs prints an access token for curl, and npm run 
    test:int runs the integration tests against a running server.

  Your three notes

  1. camelCase at the boundary: serializers.ts is the only snake_case to
     camelCase mapping. Exact-key and exact-value tests cover it in the unit
     tests (serializers.test.ts) and against the live endpoint (leases.test.ts).
  2. Temporary no-role user: the test creates its own auth user, checks 403 on
     both endpoints and an empty Data API result, deletes the user, and confirms
     the deleted user's token returns 401. Afterwards auth.users is back to 7,
     and the seeded state is exactly the brief's data.
  3. Tenant 0-rows kept: the probe on seeded data shows memberships 0 and
     audit_log 0 for all three tenants.

  Verification

  - Unit tests: 130 pass in each of UTC, Asia/Kuala_Lumpur and America/New_York.
  - Integration tests: 68/68 against the dev server and again against the
    production build. They cover:
    - 401: no credentials, garbage token, Basic scheme, bare Bearer, the
      publishable key.
    - The cookie path, and Bearer-over-cookie precedence.
    - 403 for staff and tenants.
    - 26 invalid-body cases returning 400 (never 500).
    - 5 cases returning 404 (another org's unit or tenant, an unknown id, the
      nil UUID).
    - 5 overlap cases returning 409, including both inclusive boundary days.
    - Two adjacent leases accepted.
    - 6 simultaneous creates on one unit: exactly one 201 and five 409. The
      winner has its full schedule and the losers leave no invoices.
  - Probe on seeded data: 478 checks, 0 failures (tenant isolation now tested on
    real leases). The DB constraint checks pass 26/26.
  - Production build: the secret-key grep of .next/static finds nothing.

  Real curl responses as seeded users: no token → 401 Not signed in; Ben on
  Aisyah's lease → 404; Beta manager on a Northwind lease → 404; staff POST →
  403; year 0000 → 400 startDate: year 0000 is not supported; Feb 30 → 400
  startDate is not a real calendar date: 2026-02-30; Beta's unit → 404 Unit not 
  found; overlap on Aisyah's last day → 409.

  Findings (logged in docs/notes.md)

  - Test URL clash: my integration helper read BASE_URL, which Vitest sets to /.
    All 68 tests failed with //api/leases. It's now APP_URL.
  - Stale dev server: TaskStop only killed the npm wrapper, so the old next dev
    kept port 3000, then next build overwrote .next and the "production" smoke
    test got 500s. npm start printed EADDRINUSE, which exposed it. I killed the
    process by PID, rebuilt, and re-ran everything against the real production
    server.
  - requireRole narrowing: I caught my own asserts typing before it caused
    trouble. It would have narrowed StaffActor to never, so it's now a plain
    void check.
  - Junk assertion: I removed a leftover drafting line in the overlap test and
    replaced it with a real check that a rejected request leaves no lease or
    invoices.

  Decisions to confirm

  1. receivedAt format: the API writes instants with a +08:00 offset (e.g.
     2026-03-01T00:30:00.000+08:00). The brief only says "ISO 8601 with an
     offset", so Z would also qualify. Tests comparing parsed instants pass
     either way; a string comparison would not.
  2. No lease-length cap: a 9999-year lease would try to insert about 120,000
     invoices in one transaction, which is slow but runs (30 s timeout). I
     didn't invent a limit.
  3. Ids in the body: they match a plain hex pattern, not z.uuid(), so the nil
     UUID gets 404 and a non-uuid string gets 400.
  4. proxy.ts only refreshes cookies for now. The login redirect lands with the
     UI.

  Not covered yet: Server Actions and server components (Phase 6), plus payments
  and the webhook.
